# Candidate review — 2026-10-21

From `literature/candidates/2026-10-21.json` (675 discovery, 7002 citing). Section boundary at `--min-score 3.0`.

Tick one decision per candidate and fill in the notes, then run `python literature/03_record.py`. Accepting a citing paper proposes an `entity_paper` role; the `evidence_tier` that follows is derived by `scripts/05_validate.py`, never set here.

---

## Discovery candidates (675)

New resources from the eight queries in `search_queries.yaml`. Scored for scope, with the table chosen by the query's `expect_type` — a database and a sequence model cannot share one (design §1.4).

**472 need a decision. 203 do not** — a candidate below `--min-score 3.0` is recorded as deferred rather than asked about. It is still written down in full: §1.5 measured that a filter discards more known-good papers than noise, and an unrecorded rejection returns as a candidate next cycle. What changed is only whose attention it needs, which is the same argument as list A's saturated roles.

## List A — names the entity (134)

Papers citing a catalogued `primary` paper *and* naming the entity. The two lexicon sub-scores are printed next to every proposed role, because the split is sometimes a coin-flip (design §2.6.4).

**31 need a decision. 103 do not** — `evidence_tier` saturates, so once an entity has one independent evaluation no further row can move it. Author overlap decides which half a proposal lands in; it is still not an input to any score (§2.4). A proposal with no author list always needs a human, because independence cannot be computed from nothing.

## List B — names a class, no catalogued model (172)

Deduplicated and global, because one such paper is evidence for several entities at once. This list exists because the gate that ranks named cases well discards **both** catalogued critiques of Enformer: neither names it (design §2.3). Each entry lists the catalogued primary papers it cites, so a curator can see which entities it might attach to.

**126 need a decision. 46 do not** — the same saturation test as list A, applied across every entity the paper could attach to. One target whose tier could move is enough to need a human.

### Needs a decision (126)
